       (3) BOUNDARY        (4) PERIOD
     WORKED: "~15,000" = book-era, ARC-WIDE, several administrations
             "9,575 / 37,465 sq km" = GSI SP-34, 2009, INDIA, cited by
                the Ministry of Jal Shakti, 20 March 2025
             -> NOT a decline. A DIFFERENT COUNT OF A DIFFERENT AREA.
     COROLLARY 1: a SUPERLATIVE without an inventory, a method and a

     COROLLARY 2: a COHORT change (477 -> 902 -> 2,843) is a change in
             the MEASUREMENT, not in the world.
     COROLLARY 3: an UNCERTAINTY LARGER THAN ITS MEAN is the FINDING.

# COMPLETE CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

⚠️ Compressed for retrieval. This section changes form and does not repeat the lessons' explanatory prose. Nineteen headings, one per lesson, each named for what it actually contains.

## A. Ice as a Geomorphic Agent, and the Flow Threshold

⚠️ **Ladder:** fresh snow → settled snow → **FIRN** → glacier ice; density about **0.05 → 0.83-0.9 g/cm³**; pore spaces **seal into isolated bubbles** → **impermeable crystalline solid**. ⚠️ Processes: **compaction · sublimation and re-deposition · melt and refreeze · recrystallisation**. ✅ Majid Husain: a glacier is *"a mass of ice formed from compacted, recrystallised snow that is thick enough to flow plastically."* ✅ **Firn** survives at least one summer melt season; ✅ **névé** is *"A less widely used term for firn."* — ❗ use **firn**. ❗ **Snowfield ≠ glacier; the discriminator is FLOW.** ❗ **No numerical thickness threshold is asserted.** ✅ Global frame, book-era, printed p.2.30: **~15 million km² ≈ 10 per cent of land**; **> 96 per cent of glacier ice in Antarctica and Greenland**; max measured thickness **4,270 m**. ⚠️ Therefore **global ice and Asian ice are different research objects**. ✅ Snow-line = *"the altitude which delimits the lower level of permanent snow"*, varying with **latitude, altitude, continentality**. ⚠️ **Six ways ice differs from water:** slow and lagged · **unsorted** load · debris at **every** level · **fills the cross-section** · can erode **below base level** · has **meltwater as a second, sorting agent**. ⚠️ **Two existence conditions:** accumulation surplus + thickness for plastic flow. ❗ **Cold alone is insufficient** — the high arid Trans-Himalaya proves it. 🇮🇳 India has **mountain ice only**; no ice cap, no ice sheet.

## B. The Balance Ledger and the Four Altitudes

⚠️ **Mass balance = gains − losses over a balance year** (minimum to minimum). ⚠️ **Gains:** snowfall · avalanche delivery · wind redeposition · refreezing of percolating meltwater. **Losses:** surface melt · sublimation · calving · wind scour · basal and internal melt. ⚠️ **ELA = the zero line**: positive → ELA falls → snout advances; negative → ELA rises → snout retreats. ⚠️ A rising ELA **shrinks accumulation and enlarges ablation** — a feedback inside the ledger, and the engine of peak water. ❗ **"A RETREATING GLACIER IS STILL FLOWING FORWARD."** Retreat = snout position, not flow direction. ⚠️ **A stationary snout is not a static glacier.** ⚠️ **Thinning ≠ retreat.** ⚠️ **Response is lagged and size-dependent** — small, steep, low glaciers in years; large, thick, high, debris-covered ones over decades; ❗ no response time quoted. ⚠️ **Debris double effect:** thin cover **lowers albedo and accelerates**; thick mantle **insulates and suppresses**; ❗ no critical thickness quoted. ❗ **FOUR ALTITUDES, NEVER MERGED:** **climatic snowline** (a property of the climate) · **firn line** (of the surface) · **ELA** (of the balance) · **snout** (of the geometry). 📰 ✅ Ministry of Earth Sciences, **31 July 2025**: cumulative **−26 m water equivalent, 1975-2023**; *"accelerated **heterogeneous** mass loss."* ❗ The same reply prints upper Chandra annual balance **without a sign** — quote with agency, basin and period; **do not assert the sign**. ⚠️ The identity **forbids single-cause answers**: temperature acts on the loss side only.

## C. Flow Mechanics and the Thermal Regime

⚠️ **Three components:** internal deformation (creep) · **basal sliding** · soft-bed deformation; surface velocity is their sum. ⚠️ Sliding needs a **water film**; obstacles are passed by **regelation** (melt up-ice, refreeze down-ice) and enhanced creep. ❗ **Erosion is a property of SLIDING ice.** **Warm-based → plucks and abrades; cold-based → preserves relief.** ⚠️ **Velocity field:** fastest at the **centre-line** and **surface**, slowest at **margins** and **bed**. ⚠️ Differential velocity **sorts surface debris into flow-parallel lines** → medial moraines. ⚠️ The **rigid upper zone cracks in tension → crevasses**, whose pattern maps the stress field. ✅ **Bergschrund** = *"A deep, tensional crevasse formed around the head of a cirque glacier"*; ✅ **icefall**; ✅ **pinnacle/serac**. ⚠️ **Surging:** long quiescence → order-of-magnitude velocity rise → rapid mass transfer and terminus advance; the trigger is an **internal basal-drainage switch**, not climate. ❗ **An advancing terminus is not evidence against regional loss.** 🇮🇳 ⚠️ Himalayan lower reaches **inferred** warm-based from the erosional and moraine record; ❗ **no source in this lane states a thermal regime for any Indian glacier.** ⚠️ No velocity partition is asserted.

## D. The Ice-Mass Family and the Floating Rule

⚠️ **Seven members:** cirque · valley/alpine · ice field · ice cap · ice sheet · piedmont · ice shelf with its icebergs. ⚠️ **Organising axis: does the relief steer the ice, or does the ice steer itself?** Relief-controlled ice **sharpens** relief; self-controlled ice **smooths and scours**. ⚠️ **Ice field** = relief buried but still steering; **ice cap** = dome flowing **radially outward**; **ice sheet** = continental scale, today **Greenland and Antarctica only**. ✅ Book-era scale: **Antarctic ~2,000 m over ~8 million km²**; **Greenland ~3,000 m at the dome over ~1.3 million km²**; valley type named for **Himalaya, Rockies, Andes, Alps**. ❗ **MELTING FLOATING ICE DOES NOT DIRECTLY RAISE SEA LEVEL**; grounded ice transferred to the ocean does. ❗ **BUTTRESSING, in two steps:** shelf loss removes back-pressure → grounded ice accelerates across the grounding line → *that* transfer raises sea level. 🔁 Sea-level mechanism and coasts → coastal owner; Arctic and Antarctic climate, sea ice, tundra and polar governance → polar owner (which owns the **2021 General Studies Paper I Question 15** and **2026 General Studies Paper I Question 5** demands). 🇮🇳 India has **three of seven** types. 📰 ✅ The three official impact pathways: **runoff seasonality · lake formation and outburst frequency · rising sea level globally** — ❗ rank by certainty, sea level **last**. ⚠️ Field/cap/sheet thresholds are **conventional**.

## E. The Erosional Engine and Its Diagnostics

✅ **Erosion = plucking + abrasion.** ⚠️ **Plucking:** meltwater enters joints → **refreezes on the low-pressure lee** → bonds ice to rock → the block is dragged out; ❗ needs **jointed rock** and **basal sliding**. ⚠️ **Abrasion:** entrained debris files the bed; ❗ needs **tools**, **normal pressure** and **tool renewal**; the ice alone abrades almost nothing. ⚠️ **Marks:** polish (fine flour) · **striations = FLOW DIRECTION** · grooves (large tools) · **rock flour** (the milky stream). 🔁 **Frost shattering is weathering**, owned elsewhere; used here as the **supraglacial debris supply** and as the reason **summits are angular while surfaces below are smooth**. ✅ **Roche moutonnée** = asymmetric bump, **one side ice-moulded, the other steepened or cliffed** — *"the hallmark of glacial erosion."* ❗ **Gentle, polished, striated side faces UP-ICE.** ⚠️ **Pressure melting and regelation** give one bump both treatments. ✅ **Crag-and-tail** = resistant mass protecting softer material in its lee. ⚠️ **Subglacial meltwater is under pressure and can run uphill**, driven by the **ice-surface** gradient. ❗ **Selectivity:** no sliding → no erosion → relief preserved; erosion concentrates in the **trunk**, can **over-deepen closed basins**, and follows **structure**. 🇮🇳 **Rock flour** colours glacier-fed headstreams; ❗ **sediment yield is NOT a measure of glacial erosion** — glacial, weathering, mass-movement, fluvial and anthropogenic components are inseparable in a bulk figure. ⚠️ No plucking-abrasion partition is asserted.

## F. The Upland Suite as One Process at Different Stages

✅ **Cirque** = *"amphitheatre-shaped depression at the head of a glacial valley, excavated mainly by ice-plucking and frost wedging"*; ✅ **cwm** (Welsh), **corrie** (Scottish), **cirque** (French). ⚠️ Sequence: **sheltered snow patch → bergschrund opens → headwall frost wedging and plucking → rotational abrasion deepens the floor → a ROCK LIP survives at the outlet**. ❗ The lip is why the basin holds water. ✅ **Tarn** = *"A small mountain lake created by glacial action."* — ❗ **EROSIONAL**, contrast the **moraine-dammed** lake. ✅ **Arête** = sharp ridge between **two** cirques; **French for "fish-bone"**. ✅ **Col** = a **breached arête**; ⚠️ many high passes are cols. ✅ **Horn** = pyramidal peak with **three or more** steepened faces, cirques encroaching from all sides; ✅ type example the **Matterhorn**. ✅ **Nunatak** (Inuit) = peak above an ice-sheet, **angular above, ice-moulded below** — ❗ that contrast is a **TRIMLINE** recording former ice thickness. ✅ **Crag-and-tail**. ⚠️ **Cirque orientation is a palaeoclimatic indicator.** ⚠️ **One generating sentence: the suite is one process at different degrees of completion.** 🇮🇳 ✅ **Traill's Pass** at the end of the **Pindari Glacier**, linking **Pindari to Milam**. ❗ **No source in this lane names an Indian cirque, tarn, arête or horn** — describe the setting; invent no type-locality. ⚠️ The excavation partition is unsettled.

## G. The Valley Suite, Over-Deepening and the Fjord

❗ **Discriminator:** a river occupies a **channel within** a valley; a glacier occupies **the valley**. ⚠️ **Truncated spurs** — ice is too rigid and wide to wind between interlocking spurs. ✅ **Hanging valley** = *"A tributary valley whose floor is discordant with the floor of the main valley"*; ⚠️ waterfall at the lip. ❗ **A GLACIER CAN ERODE BELOW ITS OWN OUTLET LEVEL** because the driving gradient is the **ice surface**, not the bed; **a river cannot cut below base level**. ⚠️ **Rock basins → ribbon lakes**, sometimes with a terminal moraine as a second dam. ✅ **Fjord / fiord** = a **drowned glaciated valley**; ❗ use **fjord**. ✅ **Most fjords lie on the WEST coasts of continental masses, linked to the prevailing westerlies**; ✅ distinguished by **great depth**; ✅ with an **over-deepened middle deeper than the water at the mouth**. ⚠️ Four-step derivation: **over-deepen below sea level → threshold survives at the mouth as the ice thins → deglaciation → eustatic drowning**. ❗ **Fjord against ria: is the middle deeper than the mouth?** Ria = drowned **river** valley, dendritic, deepening seaward, any latitude. ⚠️ **Picturesque, mechanically:** sheer walls · truncated spurs · great depth close inshore · **waterfalls from hanging valleys** · successive enclosed reaches · arête-and-horn skyline · sheltered water behind the sill. ⚠️ Fjord coasts also need **mountainous relief at the sea** and **Pleistocene glaciation** — the westerly argument is contributory, not sufficient. 🔁 Relative sea level and coasts → coastal owner. 🇮🇳 ❗ **India has no fjord coast**; the analogues are **high Himalayan U-troughs and hanging valleys**. ❗ **[Carries M2, 2023 General Studies Paper I Question 6.]**

## H. Direct Glacial Deposition and the Moraine Family

✅ **Till** = a **Scottish** word; material deposited **directly** by ice. ✅ **Moraine** = *"any landform directly deposited by a glacier or ice sheet."* ⚠️ **Drift** = all glacial plus glaciofluvial deposits; **stratified drift** = the water-sorted part. ❗ **Till is unsorted and unstratified; outwash is sorted and stratified** — the Core owner's named trap. ✅ But moraines are **"often partly stratified, since some may have been formed under water"** — ❗ state the rule, then the exception. ⚠️ **Moraine against loess** = unsorted till ridge against wind-deposited silt; 🔁 loess → arid-landforms owner. ⚠️ **Six moraine types:** **lateral** (records former **thickness**) · **medial** (two glaciers merged — count the tributaries) · **ground** (till sheet) · **englacial** (a transport state) · **terminal** (**maximum extent**, arcuate, convex down-valley) · **recessional** (**stillstands**). ❗ **The terminal moraine is the dam of a moraine-dammed lake** — the hazard chain begins here. ✅ **Drumlin** = oval mound largely of drift, formed **beneath** ice, **aligned with flow**, up to about **1 km long and 50 m relief**; ✅ widespread in **Canada, Ireland, Sweden, Norway, Finland**. ❗ **Drumlin: blunt up-ice, tapered down-ice** — the **opposite** of a roche moutonnée. ⚠️ Drumlins occur in **swarms** and are an **ice-sheet** bed landform. ⚠️ **Erratic** = boulder foreign to its bedrock; establishes **source and transport direction**. 🇮🇳 ✅ **Ancient moraines lie below present snouts.** 🇮🇳 ✅ **Sonapani: a dry lake basin about 2.5 km by 1.5 km just below the ice cliff.** ❗ **India has no classic drumlin field** — name the absence. ⚠️ The drumlin mechanism is unsettled.

## I. The Glaciofluvial Suite and the Sorting Gradient

❗ **The rule:** ice as carrier → **unsorted** till and moraine; water as carrier → **sorted, stratified** esker, kame, outwash, varve. ✅ **Esker** = sinuous **coarse-gravel ridge** from a **subglacial meltwater stream**, **up to hundreds of km long and 100 m high**, **parallel to ice flow**. ❗ An esker is **the cast of a river, not the valley of one** — it crosses divides because the driving gradient was the **ice surface**. ✅ **Kame** = irregular mound of **stratified glaciofluvial sediment** formed during **ice stagnation** (**Scottish**); ⚠️ **kame terrace** = a bench between ice margin and valley wall. ❗ **Kame terrace against lateral moraine: same position, opposite sediment.** ⚠️ **Kettle hole** = collapse where **buried ice melted out**; ❗ the same mechanism weakens a moraine dam. ✅ **Outwash plain** = glacial plain **at or beyond the ice margin**, often **hundreds of metres thick**, built by **aggrading braided meltwater channels**; ✅ **sandur** = an extensive plain of glaciofluvial sands and gravels in front of an ice margin. ⚠️ Cause of braiding: **sediment overload plus fluctuating seasonal and diurnal discharge**; 🔁 braiding theory → running-water owner. ⚠️ **Sorting gradient:** coarse, poorly sorted, steeply bedded **proximal** → fine, well sorted, flat-bedded **distal**. ⚠️ **Varve** = summer light band + winter dark band = **one year**; ❗ gives **duration and sequence, not calendar age**. 🇮🇳 ✅ **Karewas** = *"flat-topped terraces... consisting of clays, sands, silts together with lenticles of conglomerate from old deltaic fans."* ❗ **India has no sandur, esker or kame fields** — those need an **ice-sheet margin on a lowland**.

## J. The Indian Quaternary Record

✅ **Quaternary = Pleistocene + Recent ≈ one million years**; ✅ **the Recent began about 12,000 years ago** with the withdrawal of the last glaciation. ⚠️ **Glacial** = cold expansion; **interglacial** = warm contraction. ❗ **We are in an interglacial**, not after the ice age. ✅ **de Terra and Paterson: four glacials, three inter-glacials** — ❗ name the authors; ❗ treat as the **classical Indian framework**; ❗ **attach no absolute dates**. ✅ **First Glacial and Inter-glacial = Lower Pleistocene in Kashmir**; **Second = Middle Pleistocene** with **heavy boulder-fan and fluvio-glacial deposition**; **Upper Pleistocene = Third and Fourth glaciations plus the Third Inter-Glacial**. ✅ **Karewas "supposed to be of the second inter-glacial period"** — ❗ keep the hedge. ✅ **Narmada alluvial deposits** of the same period. ✅ **Pir Panjal uplift during the First and Third Glacials**; ✅ **Kashmir terrace deposits at the end of the Fourth Glaciation**. ✅ During glacials **sea level stood about 100 m lower**. ❗ **Eustatic = water volume; isostatic = land height.** ⚠️ **Post-glacial eustatic rise drowned the troughs that became fjords.** ⚠️ **Periglacial margin** — frost shattering, frozen ground, solifluction; 🔁 owned by the weathering owner, used here as a **debris supply**. 🇮🇳 ✅ **Ancient moraines below present snouts** are the best Indian evidence. ❗ Landforms record **that** ice was present, **not when**; no absolute age, no global correlation and no count of Himalayan advances is asserted.

## K. Where India's Ice Sits, and the Inventory That Replaces the Book Figure

✅ **Most large Indian glaciers lie in the Greater Himalaya and Trans-Himalaya — Karakoram, Ladakh, Zanskar**; ✅ the Lesser Himalaya has **only small glaciers**, traces in **Pir Panjal and Dhauladhar**. ❗ ❌ **Trap: "most Indian glaciers are in the Lesser Himalaya"** — it is too low to hold an accumulation zone. ⚠️ **Karakoram carries the greatest development despite lower total precipitation**, because almost all of it **falls and remains as snow**; ✅ a **"high, arid snowline."** ⚠️ **Syntaxial bends:** western (Hindukush-Karakoram-Pamir), eastern (Eastern Himalaya-Patkai, Arunachal Pradesh); ❗ the arc spans **Pakistan-administered territory, Nepal, Bhutan and Tibet**. ❌ ❗ **"~15,000 glaciers" is an ARC-WIDE, BOOK-ERA estimate, not an Indian count.** ✅ **GSI, *Inventory of Himalayan Glaciers*, Special Publication No. 34, 2009**, cited by the **Ministry of Jal Shakti, Lok Sabha Unstarred Question No. 3249, 20 March 2025: India has 9,575 glaciers over 37,465 km²** — **J&K and Ladakh 5,262 / 29,163 · Himachal 2,735 / 4,516 · Uttarakhand 968 / 2,857 · Sikkim 449 / 706 · Arunachal 161 / 223**. ❗ **9,575 is NOT a decline from 15,000.** ❗ **A glacier count is a function of boundary, minimum size threshold, method and year.** ❌ ❗ **Snow-covered ≠ glacierised:** books give **~33,000 km² snow-covered** of a **~five lakh km²** arc (**17 per cent**), **Karakoram ~16,000 km²**; ❗ never read 33,000 → 37,465 as growth. ⚠️ **J&K with Ladakh ≈ 55 per cent of glaciers but ≈ 78 per cent of area.** ⚠️ **Counts are threshold-sensitive; areas are robust.** 🔁 Himalayan orogeny and the thrust system → crust-and-rocks owner.

## L. The Snowline, Its Controls, and an Unresolved Contradiction

✅ **Majid Husain:** *"the altitude which delimits the lower level of permanent snow"* — **latitude, altitude, continentality**. ✅ **Khullar:** *"the lowest level of perpetual snow"* — **latitude, amount of precipitation, local topography**. ✅ **Advanced owner** adds **aspect**, and the mechanism: **greater accumulation lowers the climatic snowline; aridity raises it**. ✅ **T.D. La Touche:** latitude rises from **28° N at Kanchenjunga to 36° N in the Karakoram**, and the **eastern Himalaya rises abruptly from the plains without intervening high ranges**. ✅ The west gets much less precipitation **"but it all falls as snow."** ✅ **Sector figures:** Kanchenjunga glaciers **hardly move below 3,965 m** · Kumaon and Lahul to **~3,660 m** · Kashmir **may descend to ~2,500 m** · **Chogo Lungma terminates at 2,075 m, "the lowest recorded in the Himalayas"** (❗ Pakistan-administered). ❗ **ALL FOUR ARE SNOUT ALTITUDES, NOT SNOWLINES.** ❌ *"The snowline is the same as the lowest glacier snout"* is a named trap. ❗ **4,200 m** is a **book-era, range-wide generalisation**, on a page **partly corrupted by text bleed-through** and therefore cross-checked. ❗ ⚠️ **ASPECT IS UNRESOLVED:** Majid Husain says the snowline is **lower on southern** slopes; Khullar says **lower on northern** slopes; **both give the same reason**; no fetched official source adjudicates. ⚠️ The deciding physics: **accumulation lowers** it on the wetter side, **insolation and avalanche-shedding raise** it on steep sunny slopes — **they oppose each other**. 📰 ✅ Ministry of Environment, Forest and Climate Change, **25 July 2024**: NCPOR monitors **six glaciers in the Chandra Basin** to understand **differential glacier response**.

## M. The Glacier Map, the Basin Hooks and the Political Correction

❗ **Selection rule — twenty glaciers, not ninety:** keep a glacier only for a **basin link**, a **verified monitoring programme**, a **map-bank trap** or the **political correction**. ⚠️ **Hooks:** **Gangotri (snout Gaumukh) → Bhagirathi → Ganga** · **Yamunotri → Yamuna** · **Zemu → Tista** · **Siachen → Nubra → Shyok → Indus** · **Bara Shigri and Sonapani → Chandra → Chenab** · **Milam → Gori Ganga → Ghaghra** · **Pindari → Pindar → Alaknanda** · **Chorabari → Mandakini** · **Dokriani → Bhagirathi** · **Dunagiri, Mana, Satopanch, Bhagirath-Kharak → Alaknanda** · **Pensilungpa and Parkachik → Suru** · **Durung Drung → Doda**. ✅ Book-era scale: **Siachen 75 km, snout ~3,705 m** · **Gangotri ~30 km, snout 3,880 m** · **Milam 20 km** · **Zemu 25 km, ~1 km wide with 180 m thick ice**. ❗ ❌ **Political correction:** **Pakistan-administered** — Hispar, Biafo, Baltoro, Batura, Chogo Lungma, Punmah, Godwin Austen, Pasu, Sasaini/Ghulkin, Khurdopla, Virjerab, Mombil Yaz, Yazghil, Malangutti Yaz, Kunyong/Lak, Kyagar, Urdok, Gasherbrum; **Tibet** — Rongbuk, Kangshung; **Nepal** — Khumbu, Yepokangara, Lidanda, Chhuling, Mayondi, Annapurna; **Tajikistan (Pamir)** — Fedchenko. ❗ ❌ **Fedchenko is not Karakoram**; and **450 km² is attached to Fedchenko by Khullar and to Siachen by Majid Husain** — report the clash, pick no winner. ❗ ❌ **"nth longest outside the polar region" rankings are excluded as assertions** — ❗ **a superlative without an inventory, a method and a date is not a fact.** ✅ **Map-bank traps:** Siachen **"not in the Greater Himalaya"** · Gangotri **"Ganga begins formally at Devprayag"** · Zemu **"not in Ladakh"** · Bara Shigri **"not the source of Bhagirathi"** · Baltoro **"it is not an Indian glacier"** · Karakoram **"distinct from the Greater Himalaya"** · Hindu Kush **"not entirely in India"** · K2 **"not in the Nepal Himalaya"** · Indira Col **"do not confuse with Indira Point"** · Khardung La **"not the route from Kashmir to Ladakh"** · Teesta **"it is not a Ganga tributary at present"** · Hubbard **"not in Greenland"** · Karakoram Highway **"not through Nathu La"**. 🇮🇳 ✅ **Traill's Pass** at the end of the **Pindari Glacier**, linking **Pindari to Milam**. ⚠️ **Kangchen → Kosi** is the book's statement; **Zemu → Tista** is corroborated three times. ❗ **A map hook is not a boundary claim.** ❗ **[Carries P1, 2019 objective demand — taught, key unavailable locally, NO key inferred.]** 🔁 Siachen's strategy and boundaries → Indian political-geography owner.

## N. Measuring Change, and the Uncertainty Discipline

❗ **Three quantities:** **snout retreat (m/a)** · **area change (km² or %)** · **mass balance (m water equivalent)**. ❗ **Never compare across them.** 📰 ✅ **Ministry of Earth Sciences, 31 July 2025:** **HKH 14.9 ± 15.1 m/a**; **Indus 12.7 ± 13.2 · Ganga 15.5 ± 14.4 · Brahmaputra 20.2 ± 19.7**; **Karakoram −1.37 ± 22.8, "comparatively minor length change"**; **cumulative −26 m w.e., 1975-2023**; **Chandra basin 2,437 km², ~6 per cent of glacial area lost in 20 years, retreat 13-33 m/yr over the last decade, Himansh since 2016**. ❗ The Chandra **annual** figures (**0.51 ± 0.28 m w.e. a⁻¹**, cumulative **3.54 m w.e. 2015-2022**) are printed **without a sign** — do not assert it. 📰 ✅ **Ministry of Jal Shakti, 20 March 2025:** **WIHG monitors ten glaciers** — Dokriani (**1991**), Chorabari (**2003**), Dunagiri (**2012**), Pensilungpa and Parkachik (**2015**), Durung Drung, Saser La and Changmolung (**2022**); ground rates **Dokriani 15-20 m/yr since 1995 · Chorabari ~9-11 m/yr 2003-2016 · Pensilungpa ~2,941 m at 5.6 m/yr, Little Ice Age to 2019 · Durung Drung ~624 m at 12 m/yr, 1971-2019 · Parkachik 210.5 m at 4.21 m/yr, 1971-2021**; area change **Suru Basin 252 glaciers / 481.32 km², −32 km² (~6 %) 1971-2017**, **Garhwal 600 → 573 km², −4.6 % 1968-2006**, **Alaknanda −18.5 km² (5.7 %)**, **upper Bhagirathi −9.1 km² (3.3 %)**; **GSI: mass balance on 9, secular movement on 83**; **Annexure-II has 83 retreat rows** — **Bara Shigri 32.4 m/yr 1962-2015 · Gangotri 18.8 m/yr 1935-1996 · Milam 20.6 m/yr 1906-2011 · Pindari 9.4 m/yr 1958-2001 · Zemu 9.0 m/yr 1907-2012 · Sonapani 17.6 m/yr 1906-2014 · Gepang Gath 45.0 m/yr 2003-2012 · Samundar Tapu 24.8 m/yr 1962-2013 · Burphu 69.7 m/yr 1963-2011 · Bilare Bange 65.2 m/yr 1963-2011**. ❗ ✅ **220 surge-type glaciers over ~7,733 km² — 43 per cent of Karakoram glacier area — including 100 previously unreported**; ✅ **Thara Kangri (Rongdo) 1973-2018: "+11.13 (Surged)."** ❗ Therefore ❌ **"all Himalayan glaciers are retreating" is false as stated.** ⚠️ **Five legitimate reasons rates disagree:** different quantities · periods · methods · boundaries and thresholds · **genuine heterogeneity**. ❗ **Five rules:** carry the **±** · carry the **period** · carry the **agency** · never compare across quantities or cohorts · **a cohort change is a change in the measurement, not the world**. ✅ Khullar on Bara Shigri: *"rough estimates put its length varying from 10 to 20 km."* ❗ **No average of Annexure-II is computed.**

## O. Water Security as a Timing Problem

✅ **Seven-step spine:** thesis (**seasonal storage service**) → mechanism (mass balance, lag) → **complication** (thinning ≠ retreat; heterogeneity) → effects in descending certainty → **balance** → response → **graded conclusion**. ⚠️ **Why the store matters:** melt peaks in warm, dry, clear conditions — **negatively correlated with rainfall**. ⚠️ **Peak water:** more runoff first (stock drawdown), then less; ❗ **an increase is the first half of a decline**; ❗ no peak-water date asserted. ⚠️ **Retreat ordering: Brahmaputra > Ganga > Indus. Dependence ordering runs the other way: Indus > Ganga > Brahmaputra** — ❗ the second is **inference** from basin aridity, not a quoted statistic; no melt share is available in this lane. ✅ **Four pathways in descending certainty:** dry-season flow reliability · **lake formation and enlargement enhancing outburst frequency** · slope instability and sediment flux · **rising sea level globally**; ✅ plus **albedo feedback**. ❗ ✅ **The balancing point:** *"monsoon rainfall, not melt, supplies the bulk of annual flow in most of these basins"* → melt matters **disproportionately in the lean season and in drought years**. ⚠️ Its three uses: **pre-empts the objection · sharpens the claim · connects to policy without crossing into it**. 🇮🇳 📰 ✅ **Chandra basin** as the worked case; ❗ its 6 per cent is an **area** figure. 🔁 Routed: slope failure · coasts · regional climate · water policy, storage, hydropower finance and demand management · response doctrine. ❗ **[Carries M1, 2020 General Studies Paper I Question 6.]**

## P. The Lake-Genesis Key and the Verified Inventories

❗ **The organising question: "what dams it?"** — answerable from an image, predicts stability, ranks hazard. ⚠️ **Seven types:** cirque/tarn (**rock**) · bedrock basin and ribbon lake (**rock**) · **moraine-dammed (unconsolidated till with buried ice — the outburst reservoir)** · proglacial (**position, not dam type**) · ice-dammed (**ice; recurrent failure**) · supraglacial (**coalescing ponds**) · thermokarst (**buried-ice collapse**). ⚠️ **Retreat manufactures lakes:** vacated over-deepened basin + an impounding moraine already in place → ponding → growth → **satellite-measurable signal**. ✅ **NRSC *Glacial Lake Atlas of Indian River Basins*, 2023: 28,043 lakes ≥ 0.25 ha — 7,570 in India, 20,473 transboundary**; by size **0.25-1 ha: 12,536 · 1-5: 10,631 · 5-10: 2,445 · 10-50: 2,132 · above 50: 299 (58 in India)**. ✅ **CWC, August 2025: 2,843 monitored above 10 ha** = **902 legacy** (477 above 50 ha since 2011; **425 of 10-50 ha added 2022**, including **40 high-priority lakes identified by the Swiss Agency for Development and Cooperation for the NDMA**) **plus 1,941 added in 2025** (**581 in India**, 1,360 transboundary). ✅ **Ministry of Jal Shakti, 8 August 2024: 902 monitored — 477 above 50 ha and 425 of 10-50 ha — June to October every year.** ❗ **477 → 902 → 2,843 is a COHORT change.** ✅ **Method: Sentinel-2 and Sentinel-1 at 10 m** in **Google Earth Engine**; the **2011 inventory** came from **AWiFS / Resourcesat-1** data of **May-November 2009**, published **August 2011**. ✅ **Five change classes**; ❗ **increase above 40 per cent → vigorous monitoring for disaster purposes**; ✅ baselines **2011 base year, 2020-2024 and 2015-2024 means, minimum of the three adopted**. ❗ ✅ **Stated limitations:** mountain shadow, snow cover, cloud cover, partly frozen and dried-up lakes; **some lakes cannot be mapped at all in a given month**. 📰 ✅ **Samudra Tapu and Gepang Gath** show *"substantial expansion in their area and volume over the last five decades (1971-2022)"*; 📰 ✅ **Vasundhara Tal 0.14 km² (1968) → 0.59 km² (2021)**, bathymetric survey **2024**, with **13 Uttarakhand lakes** under assessment; ✅ **NCPOR has monitored two pro-glacial lakes in the Chandra Basin since 2013**. ❗ **A mapped lake is not a dangerous lake**; ❗ **area is not volume**. 🔁 General lake and wetland classification → lakes-and-wetlands owner.

## Q. The Outburst Chain and the Exposure Lane

⚠️ **Chain:** retreat → meltwater ponds behind moraine or in the vacated basin → **lake grows** → **the dam is unconsolidated debris, often with buried ice, and inherently weak** → **trigger** → **breach** → **sediment-charged flood wave** down a steep valley. ⚠️ **Triggers:** rock or **ice avalanche** · **extreme rainfall** · **seepage and piping** · **earthquake** · **upstream failure in cascade**. ❗ **Breach modes are NOT triggers:** **overtopping** (governed by **freeboard**) · **piping and internal erosion** · **mechanical collapse** (classically from **buried ice melting out**). ❗ A **displacement wave** can overtop a dam **with no change in lake volume** — so **area monitoring is not a safety assessment**. ⚠️ **Three properties distinguishing an outburst from ordinary flooding:** **no meteorological precursor** · **sediment-charged**, partly a debris flow · **valley-confined**, so depth and velocity persist downstream. ❗ ❌ **Refuse single-cause attribution:** the signal is retreat, the response is **heterogeneous**, and **warming does not by itself establish an event-specific cause**; ✅ *"the exact trigger chain remains a subject of post-event research"*; ✅ outbursts are **not** caused only by earthquakes. 📰 ✅ **South Lhonak (Lok Sabha Starred Question No. 68, 7 December 2023):** water spread **100-160 ha**, June-October, **2011-2023**; maxima **169 ha (Sept 2022)** and **171 ha (Sept 2023)**. ❗ **These figures and no others.** 📰 ✅ **A C-DAC sensor installed in 2016 was damaged and collapsed after 6 months**; *"at present, there is no operational early warning system installed at South Lhonak Lake."* 📰 ✅ **The flash flood of 3 October 2023**: **Teesta-III dam washed out**; sirens sounded, **Chungthang** informed, **about 250 people** evacuated; **NDRF deployed 6 teams in Sikkim and 5 in West Bengal**, 4 October to 1 November 2023; **46 human casualties including 15 army personnel**. 📰 ✅ **Exposure, quantified (8 August 2024):** design floods under review for **probable maximum or standard project flood plus outburst flood**; **outburst studies mandatory for new dams with glacial lakes in their catchments**; the **Central Electricity Authority identified 47 dams — 38 commissioned, 9 under construction**, with **studies completed for 31**. ⚠️ **Cascading risk**; ✅ WIHG research covers **Kedarnath 2013, Chamoli 2021 and South Lhonak 2023**. ❗ **Hazard ≠ risk.** 🔁 Response doctrine and resilience policy → disaster-management owner.

## R. The Observation Stack and the Limits of Prediction

✅ **Observe:** CWC Morphology and Climate Change Directorate, **monthly June-October**, **Sentinel-2 and Sentinel-1 at 10 m** in **Google Earth Engine**; **NRSC Atlas 2023**; the **2011 CWC-NRSC inventory** from **AWiFS / Resourcesat-1** data of **May-November 2009**. ✅ **Field:** **WIHG 10 glaciers** (Dokriani since **1991**) · **NCPOR 6 glaciers in the Chandra Basin, 2 pro-glacial lakes since 2013, Himansh since 2016** · **GSI mass balance on 9, secular movement on 83** · **Divecha Centre, IISc** mapping existing and potential lakes, sites in **Sikkim and Uttarakhand**. ✅ **Programmes:** **Himalayan Glaciological Programme** (DST and MoES with WIHG, GSI, NIH, SAC, NCPOR, NIHE, IISc) · **DST Network Programme on the Himalayan Cryosphere, six projects, under NMSHE** · **Steering Committee on "Monitoring of Glacier", 9 March 2023** · **Centre for Cryosphere and Climate Change Studies, NIH Roorkee, May 2023**. ✅ **Assess:** five change classes, ❗ **increase above 40 per cent flagged**; **Committee on Disaster Risk Reduction under the NDMA with six Himalayan States and Union Territories**, identifying high-risk lakes for **direct expeditions**. 📰 ✅ **NGRMP (Ministry of Home Affairs, 4 February 2025): ₹150.00 crore** across **Arunachal Pradesh, Himachal Pradesh, Sikkim, Uttarakhand**; **₹135.00 crore central from the NDMF**, **₹15.00 crore state**; **Arunachal 45.00 / 40.50 / 4.50 · Uttarakhand 30.00 / 27.00 / 3.00 · Sikkim 40.00 / 36.00 / 4.00 · Himachal 35.00 / 31.50 / 3.50**; first instalments **₹1.83 crore and ₹8.35 crore released 17 October 2024**; **four objectives** and **four components** — **I** hazard and risk assessment with a standardised method and lake inventory · **II** monitoring and early warning · **III** site-specific mitigation · **IV** awareness and capacity building. ⚠️ **Component I first** = the inventory is the binding constraint. ⚠️ The 2016 sensor failure establishes: **the environment defeats the instrument · access is seasonal · coverage is partial by nature · an announced system is not an operating system.** ❗ ✅ **CWC, August 2025, verbatim:** *"it is not feasible to make a reliable prediction of a specific occurrence on the basis of our existing knowledge. As direct predictions cannot be made, there is an urgent need to monitor a careful selection of prioritized lakes on a regular basis."* ❗ ⚠️ **The NDMA glacial-lake guidelines are NAMED and NOT QUOTED** — existence and the derived standard operating procedure attested by the **Ministry of Earth Sciences, 31 July 2025**; the document could not be retrieved and the limitation is disclosed. 🇮🇳 ⚠️ **20,473 of 28,043 lakes are transboundary** → satellite monitoring is a **structural necessity**.

## S. The Reproducible Set, the Bridges and the One Argument

❗ **Four drawings:** **Himalayan sector map with the glacierised band** · **glacier-to-river source map** · **glacier longitudinal profile with the four-altitude control** · **outburst valley cross-section**. ❗ **One scale per figure, stated**; the six scales are **snow grain · glacier · catchment · Himalayan sector · lake dam · downstream valley**. ❗ **Administration tags on every non-Indian glacier**; ❗ **a map hook is not a boundary claim**. ⚠️ **The one argument, eight moves:** (1) a **solid that flows**; (2) an **accounting identity**, and **retreat is a snout statement**; (3) **selective erosion** and **unsorted deposition**, with flow following the **ice-surface gradient** so basins can be over-deepened; (4) India's ice in the **Greater and Trans-Himalaya**, **9,575 glaciers over 37,465 km²** on the 2009 inventory; (5) change that is **heterogeneous**, with uncertainties larger than the means; (6) a **timing-and-reliability** water risk, with **dependence ordered opposite to retreat**; (7) retreat **manufacturing lakes behind weak dams**; (8) **losses as a joint product of hazard and exposure**, with **specific prediction not feasible**. 🔁 **Five bridges:** crust and rocks (why the Himalaya exists) · running water (river systems and basin transfer) · lakes and wetlands (general classification) · coastal landforms (relative sea level and coasts) · polar climate (Arctic and Antarctic climate, sea ice, tundra, and the **2021** and **2026** General Studies Paper I demands). 🔁 **Two hard hand-offs:** Siachen's strategy and boundaries → Indian political-geography owner; **all response doctrine, early-warning governance and resilience policy → disaster-management owner, which owns four verified demands**. ⚠️ The repository's answer-worthiness audit records the cross-link cluster as **glaciation with lakes and with polar climate**. ❗ **Closing sentence:** glacier change is a change in **reliability and timing** of water and in the **population of potential hazard sources** — not in water quantity, and not the cause of any particular flood. ❗ **Every quantity in this file is an observation with a method, a boundary and a date.**

## Scope, boundary and typing

- A **Mains-focused** edition of Geography Topic 06. It preserves the **integrated topic — the world-process arc and the Himalayan-application arc in one file** — across nineteen lessons: **Arc A, Lessons 1 to 10**, the world process; **Arc B, Lessons 11 to 18**, the Himalayan application, with **every Arc-B lesson carrying at least one explicit back-reference to the Arc-A mechanism it applies, named by lesson number**; and **Lesson 19**, the integrated map and synthesis capstone.
- **Ownership stated precisely.** The repository's syllabus mapping lists the Core owner under exactly one clause — *"Salient features of world's physical geography"* (Mains General Studies Paper I) — and lists the Advanced owner under **no clause at all**. ❗ The clause naming *"changes in critical geographical features (including water-bodies and ice-caps) and the effects of such changes"* is substantively this topic's territory but is **not** mapped to either owner; the mapping file currently routes it elsewhere. ⚠️ This edition **claims the clause it is mapped to**, **references** the ice-caps clause as the demand family its Himalayan half serves, and **does not amend the mapping file**, which is a separate and separately authorised task.
- **Typing.** ✅ marks a statement carried in a named source — a canonical owner, a book page listed below, or a dated official document. ⚠️ marks analytical inference, standard synthesis, evaluation or a flagged limitation. 📰 marks a dated current or institutional item with its agency. 🔁 marks an ownership routing. ❗ marks a hard prohibition or a discipline an examiner can check. ❌ marks an error being corrected. 🇮🇳 marks an India-specific consequence.
- **Page conventions, recorded so that no citation here is mistaken for another edition's pagination.** Majid Husain, *Indian & World Geography*, uses **chapter-relative folios** of the form `<chapter>.<page>`; it is the **one text-searchable local volume** and is cited as `printed p.X.YZ (local scan PDF p.N)`. ❗ **G.C. Leong's local scan has a non-uniform printed-to-scan offset**, so Leong is cited **by local-scan PDF page** and a printed folio is given only where directly visible on a render. ❗ **D.R. Khullar's local scan carries two printed pages per scan page, stacked and rotated, and the folio offset is not uniform across the book**, so Khullar is cited by **local-scan PDF page with the folios read directly off the render**. ❗ **Majid Husain, *Geography of India*, is a non-contiguous preview extract carrying a third-party watermark, not a continuous book**; it is cited by local-scan PDF page and is used for **map assets and one prose hook only**.
- **Pages actually read, which bound quotation-grade fidelity.** Majid Husain, *Indian & World Geography*: local-scan PDF pp.**52-59** (printed 2.30-2.37) and pp.**353-356** (printed 10.16-10.19), by text extraction, with PDF 353 cross-checked because it is partly corrupted. G.C. Leong: local-scan PDF pp.**50-57**, the complete chapter, **rendered and read visually**. D.R. Khullar: local-scan PDF p.**41** (folios 48-49) and pp.**50-51** (folios 66-69), **rendered and read visually**. Majid Husain, *Geography of India*: local-scan PDF pp.**42 and 48** (printed 2.18 and 2.24), **rendered and read visually**. ❗ **No page outside this list is cited anywhere in this file.**
- **The OCR finding, which governs every book citation here.** ❗ **No optical character recognition tool is installed on this machine**, and **three of the four local books carry no usable text layer** — one of them has a text layer consisting entirely of a third-party watermark. Every quotation-grade fact drawn from those three books was obtained by **rendering the page to an image and reading it visually**. ⚠️ A render of one Leong page is clipped at the right margin; no line affected by that clipping is quoted.
- **Answer-neutrality.** ❗ No answer letter, truth marking, elimination cue or option-converging reasoning appears anywhere in this file for the owned 2019 objective demand, whose official key is **unavailable locally**, or for any routed objective demand carrying the same ledger note. ❗ **No solved previous-year model answer appears**, and every model answer here is **original** and marked as such.
- **Repository-owned assets used as controlling references and not re-derived:** the map-location bank, for the thirteen Topic-06 rows and their trap column, reproduced verbatim at Lesson 13; the revision chart, for the discrimination rows on glacier work, gorge against U-shaped valley, moraine against loess, glacial lakes, submergent coasts, snowline against snout, the separation of retreat, lake growth, trigger, exposure and uncertainty, and the instruction to refuse single-cause attribution; and the answer-worthiness audit, for the fifteen-to-twenty-mark weighting of the cryosphere theme, the stated qualification that **no retreat rates be quoted** from memory, and the recorded cross-link cluster of glaciation with lakes and with polar climate.
- ⚠️ **Qdrant was unreachable during this pass.** The governing rules make it an optional, non-blocking fallback; it is **not** a source-manifest row and it did not delay or affect generation. Recorded here for completeness only.

## Corrections and normalisations applied to source material, declared

| # | Correction | Where it appears |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | ❌ **The glacier count.** Both Indian textbooks give about 15,000 glaciers *"between the two syntaxial bends"*. That is **arc-wide and book-era**, spanning Pakistan-administered territory, Nepal, Bhutan and Tibet. The verified national figure is **9,575 glaciers over 37,465 km²**, from the Geological Survey of India's *Inventory of Himalayan Glaciers*, Special Publication No. 34, 2009, cited by the Ministry of Jal Shakti on 20 March 2025. ❗ The two are **not in conflict** and the second is **not a decline** from the first | **Visible correction box at L11**; register note K; master map 3 |
| **2** | ❌ **Fedchenko.** Khullar lists it as *"second largest"* under a **Karakoram** heading while placing it in the north-western **Pamir**. It is in the **Pamir, in Tajikistan** — neither Karakoram nor Himalaya nor Indian. ⚠️ Its stated **450 km²** clashes with the same figure attached to **Siachen** by Majid Husain; **both attributions are named and no winner is picked** | **Visible correction box at L13**; register note M |
| **3** | ❌ **Political location.** Hispar, Biafo, Baltoro, Batura, Chogo Lungma, Punmah, Godwin Austen and the Hunza, Shingshal and Shaksgam rosters are **Pakistan-administered**; Rongbuk and Kangshung are **Tibet**; Khumbu and the Nepal roster are **Nepal**. ❗ Every named glacier carries an administration tag where it is not India, and **a map hook is not a boundary claim** | **Visible correction box at L13**; map caption at L19; register note M |
| **4** | ❌ **The snowline aspect contradiction.** Majid Husain places the snowline **lower on southern** slopes; Khullar places it **lower on northern** slopes of the Great Himalaya — **and both give the same reason**. ❗ **Unresolved in this evidence base**; presented as an **open question**, with the deciding physics named and neither conclusion asserted | **Visible open-question box at L12**; register note L; master map structure |
| **5** | ❌ **The superlative rankings.** The books' *"nth longest outside the polar region"* claims carry **no inventory, no method, no boundary definition and no date**, and length rankings change with how a compound system's trunk is defined. ❗ **Excluded from teaching as assertions**; retained only as a worked illustration that **a superlative without an inventory, a method and a date is not a fact** | **Visible correction box at L13**; register note M; master map 3 corollary 1 |
| **6** | ⚠️ **One range-wide snowline figure against sector-resolved altitudes.** The single **4,200 m** figure is a **book-era range-wide generalisation**; the sector figures of 3,965 m, 3,660 m and 2,500 m are **sector-resolved**, and **several of them are snout altitudes, not snowlines** | **Normalisation box at L12**; register note L |
| **7** | ⚠️ **Snow-covered area against glacierised area.** The books' **~33,000 km²** is **snow cover over the whole arc**; the Geological Survey's **37,465 km²** is **glacierised area within India**. ❗ Different quantities over different areas; **never presented as growth** | **Correction box at L11**; register note K |
| **8** | ❌ **Single-cause attribution.** The regional signal is retreat and mass loss, but the official characterisation is **heterogeneous**, one sector's mean is indistinguishable from zero, an official annexure records a **surged** glacier, and the governing causal control requires that dam material, freeboard, slope instability, displacement waves, inflow, valley geometry, exposure, warning and infrastructure vulnerability each be tested. ❗ **Attributing a specific outburst to warming is not supported** | **Visible correction box at L17**; master map argument 2; register note Q |
| **9** | ⚠️ **Terminology.** **Névé** is the source's own *"A less widely used term for firn."* — **firn** is used throughout. **Fjord** is the primary spelling, with the book's *fiord* noted once. Book transliterations are fixed to one spelling per entity, with variants noted. **Snowline ≠ snout**, and **equilibrium-line altitude ≠ climatic snowline ≠ firn line**. **Retreat is not a reversal of flow.** **Till is deposited directly; outwash is sorted.** **Fjord ≠ ria.** **Glacial-lake hazard ≠ downstream risk** | L1, L2, L7, L8, L12, L13, L16, L17; register notes A, B, G, H, L, P, Q |
| **10** | ⚠️ **Source-page defects worked around.** The Majid Husain page carrying the arc-wide framing is **partly corrupted by text bleed-through from an adjacent table**; every numeral taken from it was cross-checked against the clean prose of printed p.10.18 or against Khullar. One Leong render is **clipped at the right margin**, and no clipped line is quoted. The *Geography of India* text layer is a **watermark only**, so that book was read by render alone. The Ministry of Earth Sciences reply prints the upper Chandra basin mass-balance figures **without a negative sign** in a mass-loss passage; those figures are quoted with agency, basin and period and **their sign is not asserted** | L11, L12, L14; this ledger |
| **11** | ⚠️ **Inherited conventions re-affirmed.** The Leong printed-to-scan offset is **not uniform across the book**, so Leong is cited by **local-scan page**. The Khullar folio offset is **not uniform across the book**, so Khullar folios are **read directly off the render and never computed**. **No optical character recognition tool exists on this machine**, so three of four books were read by rendering | This ledger; every book citation in the file |

## Open uncertainties carried forward

| # | Uncertainty | How this edition handles it |
|---|---|---|
| **U1** | The **National Disaster Management Authority's glacial-lake outburst guidelines** could not be obtained as a primary document; the expected address returns a site shell rather than the document | ❗ **Named, never quoted.** Their existence and the derived standard operating procedure — covering pre-disaster preparedness, real-time emergency response and post-disaster recovery, developed with the Ministry of Home Affairs, the Department of Science and Technology and the Ministry of Jal Shakti — are attested first-party by the Ministry of Earth Sciences reply of **31 July 2025**. ❗ **No clause is quoted or characterised anywhere in this file** |
| **U2** | The **aspect-wise snowline contradiction** is unresolved: two standard texts assert opposite conclusions from identical premises and no fetched official source adjudicates | ❗ **Taught as an open question at L12**, with the deciding physics named and neither conclusion asserted |
| **U3** | The Ministry of Earth Sciences prints the upper Chandra basin mass balance (**0.51 ± 0.28 m water equivalent per year**; cumulative **3.54 m water equivalent, 2015-2022**) **without a negative sign** inside a passage about mass loss | ❗ Quoted with agency, basin and period; **the sign is not asserted**. Where a loss magnitude is needed, the unambiguous **−26 m water equivalent, 1975-2023** is used instead |
| **U4** | The repository's syllabus mapping lists the Core owner under **one** clause only, and lists the Advanced owner under **none**; the *ice-caps and their changes* clause is routed elsewhere | ❗ **Reported, not amended.** This edition claims the clause it is mapped to and references the other as a demand family |
| **U5** | Majid Husain makes the **Kangchen a tributary of the Kosi** and the Kanchenjunga Glacier's outflow; the 2025 parliamentary annexure lists **Zemu in the Tista basin**, and modern basin assignment of Kanchenjunga-flank drainage varies by flank | ⚠️ **Zemu to Tista is taught**, corroborated three times. **Kangchen to Kosi is presented as the book's statement, attributed.** Neither is generalised |
| **U6** | **de Terra and Paterson's** four-glacial and three-interglacial scheme is the classical Indian framework, and the Karewa assignment is stated by the source as *"supposed to be"* | ❗ Taught **with the authors named and the hedge preserved**, as a regional framework rather than settled modern chronology. ❗ **No absolute dates are attached** |
| **U7** | **No official key is available locally** for the owned 2019 objective demand, or for the routed objective demands carrying the same ledger note | ❗ **Absolute: no key is inferred, constructed or hinted at.** The demand is taught through the mapping it tests |
| **U8** | The **monitoring cohort changed three times** in the fetched record — 477, then 902, then 2,843 — and the comparison baselines differ by cohort (2011 base year, a 2020-2024 five-year mean, a 2015-2024 ten-year mean, and the 2023 atlas base) | ❗ Every monitoring figure is quoted with **its cohort, its base year and its report month**, and **no comparison is made across cohorts** |
| **U9** | **Qdrant was unreachable**, so no vector-retrieval cross-check of the canonical corpus was possible | ⚠️ The governing rules make it optional and non-blocking. **Not a defect, and not a source-manifest row.** Recorded for completeness |
| **U10** | Khullar states of Bara Shigri that *"rough estimates put its length varying from 10 to 20 km"* | ⚠️ Used **as teaching material at L14** — a standard text declaring its own measurement uncertain is the clearest available illustration of the date-and-data control |
| **U11** | **No melt share of flow, peak-water date, runoff projection, outburst volume, breach discharge or inundation area** is carried by any source available to this edition; nor is any thermal-regime classification for an Indian glacier, any plucking-abrasion or velocity partition, any critical debris thickness or any glacier response time | ❗ **None is asserted anywhere in this file.** Each absence is stated at the point where the figure would otherwise have been used, and the corresponding claim is written as a **mechanism with a direction** rather than as a quantity |
